# How a lesson is shaped and pitched (PF): independent check of the list

The independent check (step 2 of `streamline-plan.md`) of
`plans/2026-09-23-preferences-rest-ledger.md`, the `PF-` list of 1,130 rows.
Checked against the plugin working tree of 24 September 2026 (lesson-v4 4.2.287,
commit `79426973`, with the uncommitted 4.2.288 on top). Nothing in the plugin
or in any ledger was changed. Working files are in
`plans/streamline-tools/scratch/pfchk/`: the `missed_*.md` batch tables (seven
from the first checker, which a usage limit stopped, and three written for this
check: `slide`, `subj`, `misc`), and this check's own scripts and notes, named
`chk2_*`.

## In short

- **About 650 rules the list does not have.** The seven file batches the
  builder reran on a lighter model missed far more than one rule in ten: the
  slide composition playbook has 10 rows and 73 more places; `templates.md` has
  11 rows and 83 more; the picture guide 7 and 37 more; the history file 12 and
  45 more; the worksheet designer 8 and 37 more; the adaptation designer 4 and
  32 more. 649 places in all (about 480 marked sure), plus 10 judged outside
  this list. Every quote was machine-checked against the working tree: 657 of
  659 are exact, and the other two need re-copying (listed in 1.0).
- **Ten real pulls the decisions never raise**, the biggest being that the
  picture guide tells the decorator a subject's sensitivity "never zeroes" the
  decoration layer, the opposite of "on serious material, leave decoration
  out"; that your preferences put a word bank after the work on a slide while
  their own Worksheets section puts it with the question; and that the one
  worked example of a launch pair in `templates.md` uses the very difference
  line your preferences name as the one that fails.
- **Of the fourteen decisions, five are real pulls** (5, 7, part of 9, 11 and
  13a/b). Six are already settled by your own words or fold without a change
  of meaning (1, 2, 4, 8, 10, 12), and so is 13c; decision 3 is one real
  point inside two corrections, 6 is settled but its suggestion rests on a
  wrong fact, and 14 is a list of corrections.
- **52 of the 99 "plain repeats" are not plain repeats.** Five name the wrong
  original, and over twenty carry a condition or example their original lacks,
  including "definite-answer" work getting an answer slide, which your
  preferences never say.
- **Code:** two of this list's sections (What a Lesson Is For, and Source and
  Scenario Integrity) are frozen paragraph for paragraph by the assumed
  knowledge pin file, which the list never mentions; the slide check refuses
  thirteen things on this list's subjects that the list does not name; and
  `durationMinutes` is read (required by the validator, shown to the reviewer),
  not "never read".
- **Stories:** three stories the list says are missing from the build log are
  in it (G13, I05, and the routing card's eleven decks).

---

## 1. Missed rules

### 1.0 How this was checked

The builder's lighter-model batches were read again whole by agents on the full
model, one per file group, each following
`scratch/pfchk/missed_rules_brief.md`; I read every table they wrote. For each
candidate a script (`chk2_verify.py`) resolved the cited file, confirmed the
quote is in it word for word, and searched the whole PF ledger for it:

- 657 of 659 quotes are in their file exactly. Two need re-copying: helper
  authoring L218 is written «stick-in-sheets-designer» in the file (the table
  has a space), and the `templates.md` L64 row mixes the table's own words
  with the file's.
- None is already a row at that place. 28 share a few words with an existing
  row (`chk2_overlap.py`); each is a twin in another file (the decorator's K48
  and the picture guide's L269, the wall's L44 and the worksheet designer's
  C49), not the same place.
- I then reopened the file at about forty of the most consequential
  candidates, and every one of them read as the table says (including the
  playbook's double item 7, the speech file's `Explain.` in blue, the picture
  guide's sensitivity line, `templates.md` L347, L422, L925 and L1786, the
  card kit's words-only rule and the history file's L85 and L105).

The "Sure?" column is the batch agent's; I changed none, and list below the
rows I would treat differently. Row counts: slide references 100, picture files
57, subject files 116, worksheet files 48, adaptation and stick-in files 82,
wall files 60, helper files 65, `templates.md` 83, the make-lesson skill and
commands 26, the builders and the rest 22. Every row, with its exact words,
group and nearest home row, is in Appendix A, in the order below.

### 1.1 The slide composition playbook and the other slide references (100; the list had 13 in these four files)

The playbook is read in full by the Slide Designer on every run, and most of it
is this list's subject: said-once, the glance, colour roles, labels, filling the
slide, decoration, answer slides.

- **L90, only here:** «Never duplicate content already shown in another slot.
  The title counts as a slot, and so do the prompts a helper prints itself»,
  and «Let an instruction add only something the title does not say». No PF row
  says a title is a slot (group Q or O).
- **L55 to L70:** the whole text of the furniture exception that PF-V18 points
  to, with its six bans, one of them the closest downstream copy of F03:
  «invent classroom routine, such as books, whiteboards, partner talk or cold
  call, unless that medium is itself part of the settled task;» (groups V, F).
- **L126:** «The main teaching surface must be the biggest or strongest thing.»
  with three named pairs; "must" where PF-K32 says "normally" (group K).
- **L151 and L153, only here:** «while I am explaining this, what are the
  children looking at? There should be one obvious answer», and «**The target is
  not less teaching on the board. It is less arriving at once.**» (group O).
- **L174 to L184:** the five-step order for a tight slide, decoration last, and
  «Nothing in this order authorises dropping a reference explicitly named by
  the source unit.» PF-K36 names "the explicit ordering" but quotes none of it.
- **L264 to L274:** the answer-slide rules PF-M63 points to have no row: «When
  the task asks for a reason, the reason is part of the answer.» (group M).
- **L439, contradiction:** «- a sparse slide decorated merely because it looked
  empty.» is a fault to fix, where PF-K07 reaches for a drawing on exactly the
  slide with no imagery and your 19 September ruling wants "many". L342 («Do
  not add P3 merely because a vocabulary card is text-only.») is the same pull.
- **L106:** «exposing a verb never turns an instruction blue; blue stays the
  question's», the same pull against PF-R04 that PF-R32 records for the visual
  profile, so a ruling on R04 must reach this line too.
- **Stale:** L155 says «§8 owns it» for staging, which is §6 (§8 is
  representation continuity); the read-back at L445 to L459 numbers two items
  7, so it says ten questions and asks eleven.
- **Speech file L45, contradiction:** the leftover `Explain.` becomes «a separate
  task line in question blue outside the bubble», where PF-R19 makes
  `Explain your answer.` black. L23 to L25 hold title rules found nowhere in
  group V («Never use a verdict-revealing title such as "Fix it" or "What did
  Priya get wrong?"», «When the source carries its own judging question, that
  question is the title»).
- **Representations file L9 and L19:** H01 and F03 together, «the slide does
  not instruct direct writing on the screen or name another surface»; L61, only
  here, the callout as the repair for a sentence about one part of a picture.

### 1.2 The picture guide, the image scout and the decorator (57; the list had 23)

- **Picture guide L287 to L292, contradiction:** «A deck-level reason - visual

- **L554 to L564, contradiction with PF-K04:** «A raised hand beside "you can
  pass" works, because a child puts a hand up.» Your preferences say that very
  statement has no thing to picture and «the statement goes on its own». See 4.2.
- **Stale relevance wording the list does not name (decision 9):** L7 to L8
  «P3 is relevant decoration» (PF-K37 starts its quote one sentence later),
  L269 to L273 «stop when the relevant subjects run out», L299 «Never use an
  unrelated drawing to reach a number», against the same file's L30.
- **Emoji, the file is split (decision 9):** L504 «P3 is Educational SVG-only. It
  has no emoji, photograph or AI-generation route.» against L251 «A relevant
  emoji lifting an otherwise text-only line is decoration and is unaffected.»
- **L655 to L661:** a drawing «may be as large as the clear space allows»,
  against PF-K07's «small» and the same file's L162.
- **Only here:** L100 to L112 defines "competes" and lists five things that do
  not; L114 «**Attention is not a resource this layer spends.**»; L120 to L129
  «Do not fade a photograph, map, diagram or other visual that carries the
  teaching.»; the scout's L81 «A true image can still be wrong when it argues
  against the lesson, shows an exceptional case as typical, or adds detail that
  conflicts with the simplified model being taught.» (group N).
- **Decorator L13 and L17, stale:** its judgement is «room, relevance and
  legibility», against the guide's "restraint rather than relevance".
- **Search guide L53 to L59, stale:** «the deck prints it under the picture»; no
  builder prints a credit, and if one did it would break PF-P08.

- **Maths L43:** the fluency beat's `thinking` line («never `complete the

- **History L58, RE L17:** the history and RE forms of "one case is not the

- **History L169, only here:** «**A clip has to be checked against what you

- **Authoring guide L17 and skill L243, pull with PF-A16:** a subject file «has no

- **His examples:** the Victorian sketch (history L23 to L32) and the Tudor

### 1.11 A sample of the files read at full strength

A script (`chk2_uncited.py`) listed every paragraph of eight fully read files
that no row of any list cites and that carries a PF word; I read those. The
lesson designer, reviewer, routes and voice guide are well covered. Real
misses found:

- `agents/lesson-designer.md` L44, the binding rule (above).
- The main-task-in-the-body rule, which the list rows five times (O24 to O28),
  has five more unrowed places: `slide-designer.md` L180 and L186,
  `teacher-slide-visual-profile.md` L49, L58 and L60 («The body must still state
  the task once.»).
- `teacher-slide-visual-profile.md` L139 (an instruction belongs with the object
  it acts on, O59's second half) and L163 («Repeated photographs, diagrams and
  references return with the same treatment», M01).
- `do-beats.md` L137 «participation management remains with the teacher» (F03).

So the full-strength batches missed a few, in the slide files most; the gap is
in the rerun batches.

### 1.12 Judged outside this list

Ten rows in the `pb` and `misc` tables are about how the plugin talks to you,
not to a child or parent: plain English, no file lists, one question at a time
(`edit-templates.md` L52 to L57, `install-helper.md` L86, `SKILL.md` L64 to L75,
the playbook L1476, the diagram anchor L111, the helper builder L81, the slide
builder L55 to L78, `computer-setup.md` L7), and em dashes in text you read
(`edit-templates.md` L2, L54, L78, L94; `SKILL.md` L4). No PF home covers them
(Written Voice is for what a child or parent reads). They are listed so a fold
of Written Voice does not claim them, and because the em dash ban is yours
"anywhere".

## 2. Duplicates that are not duplicates

All 99 rows whose kind begins "duplicate" were compared with the rows they name
and with the paragraph around them (`chk2_dups_strength.md`). **52 carry or lack
something (49 from that comparison, and R38, R53 and K37 from the batch
readers); 47 are true repeats.** The summary's "99 plain repeats" should read 47.

### 2.1 The wrong original

- **PF-M35 is not a copy of PF-M02.** It carries a grading rule M02 lacks:
  «asking a child to recognise their maths in a form they have not seen is a
  real and worthwhile demand, which is exactly why it belongs where the method
  is already secure and not where it is still being built».
- **PF-M58 is not a copy of M08 or U01.** It holds the two note markers a program
  depends on («`Answer to question(s) on this slide:`», «`Answer/model for this
  slide:`») and «The lesson-designer does not duplicate the answer/model/standard
  in `speakerNotes`». The same paragraph (output template L679) has two sentences
  in no list: «For My Turn include the exact question/example plus answer after
  the marker; for other cases include the answer/model/standard only.» and «Do
  not mechanically append punctuation to `answer.content`.»
- **PF-J11 is not a restatement of J01.** «**Never write a question number.** ...
  the engine counts them: zone `a`, then `b`, then `c` ... bold black in
  brackets.» The black differs from R01's purple slide labels.
- **PF-T44 is about the question put to the class, not the difference line:**
  «**Do not ask "which is better?"**», with three replacement asks.
- **PF-K61 is not a copy of K40:** it restricts P3 to «the exact six ordinary card
  types above», and nothing above it in that file lists six.
- **Five duplicates point at another copy, not at a home** (F78, F79 at F66; K60
  at K17; K61 at K40; R52 at R31; R53 at R40). **PF-F57** names "L21", which as a
  PF row is the grammar drill; probably SC-L21 (unsure).

### 2.2 A condition, scope or example the original lacks, not said in the row

- **PF-M61, M53, M59:** «Follow **definite-answer** work with an answer slide.»
  Your preferences (M08) give answer slides to main independent work with no
  definite-answer condition; a fold that keeps M08 loses the rule that open
  work gets none. No decision names it.
- **PF-P05** is wider than P02: «Anything today's teaching leans on from an
  earlier lesson ... gets a short reminder», not only a name; PF-G16 points to
  it as the home.
- **PF-T17, T19** lack T02's first condition («the product is the class's own work
  on what they have just built»).
- **PF-R25 pulls against R02:** «everything else the board says stays black,
  including the instructions children act on» with no starter exception, and
  adds «A category container takes its colour on the border».
- **PF-R38** is narrowed to answers; **PF-R53** adds «so a question slide and its
  answer slide can use the same helper»; **PF-R35** adds «`visible-in-unit` is
  valid only with `modellingState: "Prepared example"`».
- **PF-S26** is narrower (foundation Teach slides only) and adds «name the useful
  representation or visual treatment clearly enough for the slide-designer to
  render it faithfully».
- **PF-D55** adds a permission: «a lesson with no light moment in it is not a
  lesson with a fault». **PF-D68** drops D15's «None of those phrases is
  individually forbidden».
- **PF-C11** carries the planning-noun example («show each claim» to «find out
  each of these things»). **PF-B49** lacks B07's limit (a block already one move
  gains nothing from a break). **PF-F63** adds that the date is left blank.
  **PF-O37** adds «The example must be accurate, readable and chosen for the exact
  learning.» **PF-H19** adds a «**large**» blank representation. **PF-H26** adds «two
  PWMs per question» and «Non-maths same depth.» **PF-H28** adds «Units belong in
  the question or outside the bubble.» **PF-X04** adds the honestly written
  thinking line as the diagnostic. **PF-X59** drops «on the lesson's own material».
- **PF-K37** is the stale «P3 is relevant decoration» (decision 9), not a duplicate.
  **PF-K60** lacks three of K17's four conditions («a plain or quiet background, a
  near-square usable crop, and a subject that remains clear at print size»).
- **PF-F26, F49, F50** add marking, checking and book-look routines to F03's list.
  **PF-F53** adds two avoid items. **PF-B23** adds «accurate» and «age-appropriate»
  (the second sits awkwardly with B02's «not by reading age or word difficulty
  alone»). **PF-A28** applies to the compatibility route, which A04 never names.

### 2.3 Rows whose own kind already says they differ

that sorts by kind would treat them as covered: adds B49, C20, C24, C28, D27,

## 3. Strength and scope

About 170 rows were spot-checked against their paragraph (the list is in
`chk2_dups_strength.md`), and every row's cited line was checked by script: no
wrong line numbers. The wrong ones:

- **"When it applies" drops an exception or a limit the text gives:**
  - PF-C02: «Once a technical word has been properly taught in the lesson, start
    using it normally; a vocabulary card alone is a reference, not that teaching».
  - PF-C08 (strength must): «It may be revealed or produced live» and notes «may say
    the same thing more fully».
  - PF-O08: «Do not pre-complete a live model to make the starting slide
    self-contained.» PF-O09: «a useful explanation need not have a picture» and «do
    not invent a reason that adds no meaning». PF-S05: «when it improves
    understanding», and text may be the teaching object.
  - PF-G07: «or that one thing cannot be read from another». PF-N02: «where the
    lesson can afford it, a second case does the saying instead».
  - PF-B34: its scope sentence «This governs every contrast used to teach or test a
    category, wherever it sits». PF-Y11: «At most one boundary line per Teach
    slide» holds on every Teach slide.
  - PF-D49: drops «unless the brief gives a clear reason not to» and invents a
    maths exception. PF-D50 drops «unless the brief gives a clear reason to depart
    from it». PF-O78 drops «Combine natural pairs or tightly related visual parts
    when they form a faster lesson overview». PF-S12 and PF-O29 stop before the
    sentences that carry the rule (the caption rules; the three glance questions).
    PF-K36 and PF-Q09 name an extension their quotes do not hold (the five-step
    order; the key question kept visible).
- **Wrong strength:** PF-K07 is "may" for «Reach for it in both», which your
  19 September ruling strengthens (at least default); PF-K63, your ruling, has no
  strength or scope at all. PF-X53 to X55 are "default" for imperatives whose home
  (G11) is must. PF-N10, S10, B41, V20 and X38 are "may" but carry a must-not
  («it must be a substring of the source string», «It must not add a new task»).
  PF-U35's «the only real option for a physical skill that must be *shown*» is a
  must. PF-K13 is "must" for advice («is the cheapest access support»). Unsure:
  I02, D73, K35.
- **No strength on a rule:** PF-H02 and H22.
- **Wrong kind:** PF-R04 is stale by the list's own out-of-date table and its kind
  says "rule". PF-R28 is a contradiction (stale since 4.2.109: «every child-facing
  question and instruction carries it»), not a near-duplicate. PF-C49's quote is
  A04's copier triggers, not the C07 pointer its kind names, and it drops «Also
  read the rest when you must report that settled wording is unsuitable»; C49 and
  D31 quote the same two sentences. PF-L12 is a near-duplicate that adds «Do not
  impose more questions than Expected, fixed maths section counts or a reasoning
  quota», not a pointer. PF-S21's last sentence copies S04. PF-N23 names a case
  its text does not hold. PF-G43 is G09's test, not G03's. PF-N28 drops a slide
  title rule («the slide titles name the period») and your quoted words. PF-O32
  and P08 are called "only here" while the science and history files hold them.
  PF-K40 is not a gate (the file says it replaced one). PF-M75's premise («An answer
  slide repeats the question above its answer») is what M63, M66 and M09 say is
  optional.
- **Wrong proposed home:** F66, F78, F79 (the date and objective) go to Cognitive
  Load Triage; R27, R28, R31, R39, R52 and R29, R41 (colour) go to Question
  Labelling or Slide Headings; M34 goes to Source and Scenario Integrity. R39's
  place is `teach-compare` in section 2.3, not `compare-contrast`.
- **Homes that are stale against their copies:** PF-L02 names only Greater Depth
  (the adaptation files use the catalogue for Below); PF-J06 and J07's reason (a
  separate answer key) does not hold for a stick-in piece; PF-F07 and F72 still

- **Missing from a decision's row list:** PF-K22 (the history file's list of

## 4. Disagreements

Read against the files, with your "no overcomplications" in mind. A real pull is
two places that disagree, a rule that looks wrong, or a fold that would change a
meaning. Anything else is settled by your own words or folds as it stands.

### 4.1 The fourteen decisions

1. **Maths slide titles: settled by your words.** Your 19 September ruling is
   written into the preferences (PF-V03: «This is maths only»), and the slide
   check already lets bare `Apply` through in maths only
   (`check-slide-design.js` L100, L108). The other copies need the maths clause
   added. A correction, no question.
2. **The launch: settled by your words.** Your 18 September edits are the home, and
   two of the routes' own output blocks (T17, T19) already follow them. The
   designer (T12, T13), the reviewer (T14) and the task-centred prose (T18) are
   out of date. One thing the fold must keep: the trigger T14 and T18 add («when
   the product's form is new to the lesson or the enabling input ran to several
   units»), which the home lacks. A correction.
3. **Speaker notes: one real point.** The order is out-of-date text (the home says
   «a script first», the slide designer prints `On the board:` first on purpose)
   and naming `Look for` is a fold that adds nothing new; both are corrections.
   The sentence rule is a real pull: «short straightforward sentences» a
   «nine-year-old» follows (PF-U07) against «sentence length and vocabulary chosen
   for the idea rather than a mechanical simplicity rule» (U01). Your Written
   Voice words favour U01; the one question is whether "a nine-year-old" goes.
4. **An answer slide after a live model: settled by your words.** Your 17 September
   decision on the cover teacher's deck is in the program and the build log
   («must deliver `answer-slide`»); the preferences sentence and the skill route
   clause were never updated. A correction.
5. **Each cycle's Your Turn: real.** Both sides are yours: M08 makes every Your Turn
   with independent work "main", and your quoted words in the skill route call
   a cycle's Your Turn «a check rather than the main practice». Also see 2.2: the
   copies add "definite-answer", which bears on this. Keep.
6. **The minutes: settled by your words, with one wrong fact.** Your "30 to 50, 45
   the aim" settles the numbers. But the decision says the program «never reads
   the duration you supply»: the validator requires `durationMinutes`, and the
   reviewer is shown it («- Duration: ... minutes», `design-review-packet.py`
   L686, L2069); only the minutes check ignores it. Making the 50 follow a
   supplied duration is a new feature, not a fold; I suggest parking it. The
   history file's input share (1.3) must survive the fold.
7. **"Which book, one between two": real, and narrow.** F03 keeps the recording
   surface out of notes as well as slides; the Do-beat catalogue sends «Which book,
   which page, one between two, sitting where» to the script. Keep.
8. **Four pieces or no cap: settled by your words.** Both are yours at different
   scopes (a Teach slide; a lesson). One fact the suggestion misses: the Teach
   layouts already refuse more than a lead and four cards beside a picture
   (`teach-layouts.js`, `column: [1, 4]`, refused as `TEACH_LAYOUT_INVALID`), so
   "used as a judgement and not counted" would be untrue. The fold adds the
   pointer and says the layouts hold five at most.
9. **Decoration: half settled, half real, and wider than written.** Relevance and
   "many" are settled by your 19 September words; the picture guide contradicts
   itself on them too (1.2), so it needs the correction as well as your
   preferences. The serious-material limit is the real question, and it is worse
   than "in no file the decorator reads": the guide tells the decorator the
   subject's sensitivity «never zeroes the layer», the decorator says «a
   deck-level judgement never zeroes this layer», and the history file (PF-K22)
   lists death, persecution, enslavement and the rest as places to omit P3. The
   emoji half («one emoji per line» against «no emoji ... route») is split inside
   the guide itself.
10. **Grow or hug: settled by your words.** Your 19 September "fill the box you're
    in" decides it, and the log records the repair as waiting. But applying it
    changes what the builder does by default (`templates.md` L652 to L658 make
    `hug` the default), so it is a repair release, not a fold.
11. **The blue job line: real**, because the build log leaves it "the teacher's to
    settle". Small; one line.
12. **The research file's lines: settled.** Your precedence rule already decides
    every one, as the decision itself says. Adding clauses is optional; not a
    question.
13. **Three limits: (a) and (b) real, (c) settled.** (a) A title question showing
    its answer (Q01, a must with no exception) against a dialogic Stimulus, whose
    takeaway «lives in the children's response» (Q08): adding the exception
    changes the home. The playbook adds two more places with no exception (L328,
    L364). (b) `goodLooksLike` null «when the success criteria already show what a
    good one looks like» (T27) widens the home's exemptions. (c) The program and
    two route files say a Teach always has a `thinking` line («A Teach has a
    thought in it»), which is the later repair; a correction.
14. **Out-of-date text: corrections, one line.** Add the stale items this check
    found (4.3).

So five decisions are real pulls (5, 7, 9's serious-material part, 11, 13a
and b), 3 holds one; the rest are settled or corrections, and 6's suggestion
rests on a fact that is wrong.

### 4.2 Real pulls the list missed

Each is two places that disagree, checked in the files.

1. **Decoration on serious material** (above, 9): the picture guide L289 to L291
   and the decorator L87 against your preferences L412 and the history file L160.
   Suggest: the limit stands and both lines lose "the sensitivity of the subject".
2. **A picture beside "you can pass".** Preferences L391 (PF-K04): «"You can pass
   without giving a reason" is not an object ... The answer is the one above: the
   statement goes on its own.» The picture guide L554: «A raised hand beside "you
   can pass" works, because a child puts a hand up.» One of them has to go.
3. **Where a word bank sits.** Preferences L618 (PF-R11): «a steps list, a word bank
   or a reminder ... come after the work in the scan ... The Worksheets section
   says the same thing». The Worksheets section and the worksheet designer L157:
   a word bank «the answer is chosen from» and a step list «worked *through*» sit
   with the question, above. A fold of R11 either loses that test or keeps the
   clash inside your own preferences.
4. **Labels on stick-in pieces.** PF-J06 and J07: a stick-in piece's numbering and
   letters start again «because it is a separate resource with its own answer
   key». The stick-in designer L46 and pedagogy L79: the piece carries the board's
   own handle «so the cut-out, the board the child is working alongside, and the
   book all name the question the same way». Stick-in pieces have no answer key.
5. **Pictures on printed sort cards.** PF-K01 names «a sorting card» as a place a
   picture carries a word, and the adaptation designer (PF-K13) wants a picture
   beside each concrete word on a Below card set; the card kit «prints words
   only». Possibly a limit of the kit rather than a rule (unsure).
6. **"The words never shrink" and the build's autofit.** PF-O04: «the words never
   shrink, and they are never clipped to fit». `templates.md` L662: «The build
   shrinks it to whatever the box actually holds and stops at the 18pt
   projection floor»; the helper guide L169 and the template command L150 say
   the same. PF-Y27 and O76 read O04 as "not below the floor". A fold that keeps
   O04's words says the opposite of the build; suggest "never below the readable
   size, and never clipped".
7. **Blue on a word that is not a question; green on a wrong answer.** PF-R02:
   «Blue asks; everything else tells.» `templates.md` L1786 colours a word inside
   a statement «focus blue» («The [[tens]] column changes.»), L804 marks a table's
   deciding word the same way, and L1777 gives the callout box its own colour
   grammar. PF-R05 keeps prepared examples black; L925 prints «A worked chain the
   lesson is about to show is wrong» green.
8. **The Roman diary.** PF-I01 uses «know how a Roman lived, then write a diary
   entry as one» as a fair product for a second lesson; the history file L105
   says «A diary entry as a Roman soldier is not history» as the lesson's
   outcome. Suggest swapping the example.
9. **`Practise` as a title.** Preferences L667 lists `Practise` as a slot name to
   replace; the slide check's comment calls it a valid child-facing label and
   lets it through (`check-slide-design.js` L84 to L85).
10. **The wall, for topic 9:** condensing a child's prose as the first move (1.6)
    against B07 and O04; the wall's colour grammar against R02 and R05; five
    seconds against two. Real, but the wall files are topic 9's, and the starters
    list already asks about condensing sticky facts.

Two more read as pulls and are not: the worksheet and wall designers' "these win
over anything" (settled by the lesson designer's full order, A16, a fold with no
change of meaning), and the subject files' authority (the subject files list
notes it).

## 5. Code and tests

From `chk2_code.md`; the numbers and codes below were checked in the files.

### 5.1 Enforced or pinned, and missing from the list

- **Two sections are frozen whole by another list's test.**
  `assumed_knowledge_ledger_pins.json` records `## What a Lesson Is For` (14
  paragraphs) and `## Source and Scenario Integrity` (5) as homes, and
  `test_the_homes_hold_exactly_their_paragraphs` (`ledger_pin_checks.py` L160 to
  L173) compares them paragraph for paragraph. Any edit to groups G or N, even a
  PF-owned sentence, fails the assumed-knowledge test. The contents list is
  pinned whole three times (SC-A02, TD-A12, VOC-A03), and 194 pins from five other
  lists are bound to this list's headings: moving a phrase to another section
  fails them, not only rewording it.
- **The slide check refuses thirteen more things on this list's subjects**
  (`builder/scripts/check-slide-design.js`), none in the list:
  `INTERNAL_STAGE_TITLE` (V01, V04), `TURN_SLIDE_WITHOUT_ITS_TURN` (V02),
  `SAME_LINE_TWICE_ON_ONE_SLIDE` and `TEACH_LINE_REPEATS_TITLE` (Q02),
  `MIXED_BLOCK_WHOLE_BLUE` and `STARTER_QUESTIONS_ALL_BLUE` (R02, R19),
  `STICKY_LINE_RECOLOURED` (R20), `LAUNCH_PAIR_NEEDS_ITS_TEMPLATE` (T09, T40),
  `TEACH_SLIDE_WITHOUT_ITS_SCRIPT` (U39), `TEACH_SPLIT_LEAVES_A_LABEL` (O04),
  and the Teach layout checks that make each layout's slot caps binding. The
  Teach layouts also refuse a second orange line (R50, "must" with no code).
- **Validator limits no row records:** the launch's `difference` at most 140
  characters (`LAUNCH_DIFFERENCE_MAX_CHARS`, L269); `reasoningWords` at most 6,
  never empty, no repeats (L1933); `rehearsal` lines at most 200 characters
  (L1934); which beats must have a script (`SCRIPT_REQUIRED_KINDS`, L198; not a
  starter, Do or Your Turn); an Our Turn's script must ask a question
  (L2297); the answer markers refused inside the design's notes (L1116); a
  launch's `show` must name the beat's own picture or representation (L2217).
- **Builder behaviour:** every builder silently turns an em or en dash into a
  spaced hyphen (`shared/text/house-style.js` L23 to L30), so only the design is
  refused, not a downstream spec; question labels are drawn purple and a typed
  label that disagrees is refused (`QUESTION_LABEL_CONFLICT`); an answer slide
  is recognised only by a title ending `- check` or `Answers` (V07's forms are
  load-bearing); the starter header fills in the objective when the slide
  leaves it out.
- **Headings tests name** that the list does not list: `### Core rules`, the three
  Lesson Designer parts of Slide Philosophy, `### Speaker notes hand-off`,
  `Written Voice read-back`, both Teach slide calibration headings, `Question
  Labelling`, and «Contents — each agent reads its own sections».

### 5.2 Stated wrongly

- **`durationMinutes` "never read"** (the code section, PF-F21, decision 6): the
  validator requires it (L3467) and the reviewer's packet prints it twice. The
  minutes sum ignores it; it is also skipped entirely when any beat is untimed
  (L3331).
- **"No program enforces the four-piece Teach ceiling"**: the layouts cap a lead
  plus four cards beside a picture, refused by name. Four is not enforced; five
  is.
- **The answer-slide summary** misses that a My Turn not finished live may never
  use `answer-slide`, and that "main independent work" is a fixed list of kinds
  in code, not "when that beat contains work children complete independently".
- **Said once** is checked more widely than "the takeaway never the headline"
  (every pair of headline, takeaway, sticky facts and explanation sentences),
  and only on `teach`, not on `teach-why` or `teach-needed`, though the home says
  "the Teach slide in every route".
- **The displayed objective:** nothing enforces the stored `To`; the permitted
  cuts in code are wider than PF-F07 says (any comma, a bracket, a spaced
  hyphen, " through ").
- **PF-R40 "must (code)"**: no build refuses `{{green}}` or `||` on a teaching slide;
  `{{answer-green}}` exists nowhere in code.
- **`BLUE_WITHOUT_A_QUESTION`** lives in the slide check, not the builder, and
  fails that check like its twelve siblings.
- **Pins:** the scan path's limits are pinned (R09 to R12), and so are W06 and W07,
  though the list says they are not; the 20-character floor also misses heading
  pins. 686 is a lower bound.
- **Reading routes** name only one selector into your preferences
  (`Written Voice (House Style) > Core rules`); the worksheet designer also
  names the `Three habits` paragraph, not only the wall and stick-in designers.

Every heading the card names exists exactly. Not flagged by the list:
